## Assistant

I’ve reviewed the RaceVision material available from the previous conversations and the associated project logs in your saved conversation files. I now have the project context in place for your next question.

The key project state I’m carrying forward is:

- **RaceVision** is the F1 live telemetry → runtime → transport → 3D/Unity → OBS/broadcast → camera/director → prediction/analytics → public API platform.
- The architecture is deliberately **modular**, with clear ownership boundaries and no leakage of Runtime internals into consumers.
- The core persistence layer is **PostgreSQL + SQLAlchemy/Alembic**, with the established telemetry/race domain model.
- **Phase 10 Unified Runtime** was accepted and acts as a major timing/runtime authority.
- **Phase 11 Live Streaming/Transport** was fully accepted: its acceptance record shows **83/83 gates passed**, with LIVE/REPLAY isolation, subscriptions, connection lifecycle, outbound protection, observability, security, and integration coverage all verified. fileciteturn1file1turn1file4
- Phase 11 explicitly keeps **transport as delivery only**; it must not invent timing, interpolation, extrapolation, replay control, or duplicate Runtime/EventBus ownership. fileciteturn1file4turn1file9
- **Phase 12 Unity/3D** was developed with the important environment limitation that Unity execution/testing could not be fully performed because of the missing Unity project manifest in the earlier environment.
- **Phase 13 OBS/Broadcast** established the OBS WebSocket adapter, overlay state, stream output/adapters, and broadcast boundary, but live OBS validation remained an environment limitation.
- **Phase 14 Camera & Director** introduced the camera registry/state/decision architecture. The director remains separate from telemetry/runtime concerns, and camera-state semantics were deliberately kept distinct from higher-level director intelligence. The later agents extend this toward tracking, movement, transitions, shot selection, scoring, Unity binding and OBS control. fileciteturn1file6
- **Phase 15 Prediction & Latency Compensation** established the prediction pipeline and the planned 5-second delayed-view architecture, with acceptance achieved subject to the known live-environment limitations.
- The roadmap subsequently extends into **Phase 16 analytics/AI commentary** and **Phase 17 public APIs/external integrations**, followed by later production/deployment work.
- There is also newer saved material showing **Phase 18 production deployment work**, including production app wiring, readiness/liveness, deployment scripts, migration tooling and security hardening. The repository-side work was accepted with limitations because real Docker, PostgreSQL and WebSocket production rehearsal still required operator validation. fileciteturn1file5turn1file8
- A very recent concrete issue in the saved logs is the **FastAPI Cloud build failure** caused by setuptools discovering multiple top-level packages in the flat repository layout (`sdk`, `infra`, `shared`, `config`, `backend`, `frontend`, `database`, `rendering`, `generated`). fileciteturn0file5

I’ll also keep the distinction between **“repository/tests say it works”** and **“the real external environment has been physically verified”** front and centre. That distinction appears repeatedly throughout your acceptance reports and is important to avoid falsely declaring production readiness. fileciteturn1file3

I’m ready for the question.

## User

In Supabase there is a SQL Editor, please give me commands I can run to create a full database

## Assistant

Yes. Since RaceVision is already PostgreSQL-based, **Supabase PostgreSQL is a good fit for the database layer**. The SQL below creates a complete RaceVision foundation in the Supabase SQL Editor, including the core F1 entities, live telemetry, race sessions, laps/sectors, weather, replay, race events, camera events, statistics, indexes, constraints, timestamps, and useful database views.

**Important:** this creates the database schema in Supabase. Your existing RaceVision Alembic/SQLAlchemy models should remain the authoritative application-side schema. Before using this against an existing database, take a backup and compare it with the repository's current migrations.

### 1. Run this entire script in Supabase → SQL Editor

```sql
-- ============================================================
-- RACEVISION DATABASE
-- PostgreSQL / Supabase
-- Initial complete schema bootstrap
-- ============================================================

BEGIN;

-- ============================================================
-- 0. EXTENSIONS
-- ============================================================

CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;

-- ============================================================
-- 1. HELPER FUNCTION
-- ============================================================

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at = NOW();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;

-- ============================================================
-- 2. ENUM TYPES
-- ============================================================

DO $$
BEGIN

    IF NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM pg_type WHERE typname = 'race_session_status'
    ) THEN
        CREATE TYPE public.race_session_status AS ENUM (
            'SCHEDULED',
            'PRACTICE',
            'QUALIFYING',
            'SPRINT',
            'RACE',
            'COMPLETED',
            'CANCELLED'
        );
    END IF;

    IF NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM pg_type WHERE typname = 'race_session_mode'
    ) THEN
        CREATE TYPE public.race_session_mode AS ENUM (
            'LIVE',
            'REPLAY'
        );
    END IF;

    IF NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM pg_type WHERE typname = 'driver_status'
    ) THEN
        CREATE TYPE public.driver_status AS ENUM (
            'ACTIVE',
            'INACTIVE',
            'RESERVE',
            'TEST'
        );
    END IF;

    IF NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM pg_type WHERE typname = 'telemetry_source_type'
    ) THEN
        CREATE TYPE public.telemetry_source_type AS ENUM (
            'LIVE',
            'REPLAY',
            'API',
            'MANUAL',
            'SIMULATION'
        );
    END IF;

    IF NOT EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM pg_type WHERE typname = 'replay_status'
    ) THEN
        CREATE TYPE public.replay_status AS ENUM (
            'CREATED',
            'RECORDING',
            'COMPLETED',
            'FAILED',
            'ARCHIVED'
        );
    END IF;

END
$$;

-- ============================================================
-- 3. TEAMS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.teams (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    short_name TEXT,
    constructor_code TEXT,
    country_code CHAR(3),

    primary_color TEXT,
    secondary_color TEXT,

    logo_url TEXT,

    is_active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_teams_name UNIQUE (name)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_teams_active
    ON public.teams (is_active);

-- ============================================================
-- 4. DRIVERS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.drivers (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    permanent_number INTEGER,
    first_name TEXT NOT NULL,
    last_name TEXT NOT NULL,

    abbreviation CHAR(3),

    nationality TEXT,
    country_code CHAR(3),

    date_of_birth DATE,

    status public.driver_status NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ACTIVE',

    team_id UUID REFERENCES public.teams(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    headshot_url TEXT,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_drivers_permanent_number
        UNIQUE (permanent_number),

    CONSTRAINT uq_drivers_abbreviation
        UNIQUE (abbreviation)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_drivers_team
    ON public.drivers (team_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_drivers_status
    ON public.drivers (status);

-- ============================================================
-- 5. CARS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.cars (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    team_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES public.teams(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    car_number INTEGER,

    chassis_name TEXT,
    engine_supplier TEXT,

    model_name TEXT,

    active_from DATE,
    active_to DATE,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_cars_car_number
        UNIQUE (car_number)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_cars_team
    ON public.cars (team_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_cars_driver
    ON public.cars (driver_id);

-- ============================================================
-- 6. TRACKS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.tracks (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    short_name TEXT,

    country TEXT,
    country_code CHAR(3),

    latitude DOUBLE PRECISION,
    longitude DOUBLE PRECISION,

    length_m DOUBLE PRECISION,
    corner_count INTEGER,

    elevation_m DOUBLE PRECISION,

    track_map_url TEXT,
    blender_asset_path TEXT,
    unity_asset_path TEXT,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_tracks_name UNIQUE (name)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_tracks_country
    ON public.tracks (country_code);

-- ============================================================
-- 7. RACE SESSIONS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.race_sessions (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    track_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES public.tracks(id)
        ON DELETE RESTRICT,

    name TEXT NOT NULL,

    season INTEGER NOT NULL,

    round INTEGER,

    session_type TEXT NOT NULL,

    status public.race_session_status
        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'SCHEDULED',

    mode public.race_session_mode
        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'LIVE',

    start_time TIMESTAMPTZ,
    end_time TIMESTAMPTZ,

    scheduled_start TIMESTAMPTZ,

    total_laps INTEGER,
    lap_distance_m DOUBLE PRECISION,

    weather JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    configuration JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_sessions_season_round
    ON public.race_sessions (season, round);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_sessions_track
    ON public.race_sessions (track_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_sessions_status
    ON public.race_sessions (status);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_sessions_start
    ON public.race_sessions (scheduled_start);

-- ============================================================
-- 8. TELEMETRY SAMPLES
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.telemetry_samples (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    car_id UUID REFERENCES public.cars(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    source public.telemetry_source_type
        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'LIVE',

    sequence_number BIGINT,

    -- Track-space coordinates
    x DOUBLE PRECISION,
    y DOUBLE PRECISION,
    z DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- World-space coordinates
    world_x DOUBLE PRECISION,
    world_y DOUBLE PRECISION,
    world_z DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Velocity
    speed_kmh DOUBLE PRECISION,

    velocity_x DOUBLE PRECISION,
    velocity_y DOUBLE PRECISION,
    velocity_z DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Motion
    heading_deg DOUBLE PRECISION,
    yaw_deg DOUBLE PRECISION,
    pitch_deg DOUBLE PRECISION,
    roll_deg DOUBLE PRECISION,

    acceleration_x DOUBLE PRECISION,
    acceleration_y DOUBLE PRECISION,
    acceleration_z DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Distance / track position
    track_distance_m DOUBLE PRECISION,
    lap_distance_m DOUBLE PRECISION,
    progress DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Race state
    lap_number INTEGER,
    sector_number INTEGER,

    position INTEGER,

    gap_to_leader_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    gap_to_ahead_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Tyres
    tyre_compound TEXT,
    tyre_age_laps INTEGER,

    -- Car state
    throttle DOUBLE PRECISION,
    brake DOUBLE PRECISION,

    drs_enabled BOOLEAN,
    ers_level DOUBLE PRECISION,

    fuel_load_kg DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Timing
    lap_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    sector_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    -- Raw source payload
    raw_data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    -- Prediction/interpolation support
    interpolation_state JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_telemetry_session_time
    ON public.telemetry_samples (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_telemetry_driver_time
    ON public.telemetry_samples (
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_telemetry_session_driver_time
    ON public.telemetry_samples (
        race_session_id,
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_telemetry_sequence
    ON public.telemetry_samples (
        race_session_id,
        sequence_number
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_telemetry_lap
    ON public.telemetry_samples (
        race_session_id,
        driver_id,
        lap_number
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 9. LAPS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.laps (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    lap_number INTEGER NOT NULL,

    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,

    lap_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    sector_1_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    sector_2_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    sector_3_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    position INTEGER,

    pit_stop BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    tyre_compound TEXT,
    tyre_age_start INTEGER,

    fuel_used_kg DOUBLE PRECISION,

    is_personal_best BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    is_fastest_lap BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    invalidated BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_lap_session_driver_lap
        UNIQUE (
            race_session_id,
            driver_id,
            lap_number
        )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_laps_session
    ON public.laps (race_session_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_laps_driver
    ON public.laps (driver_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_laps_time
    ON public.laps (lap_time_ms);

-- ============================================================
-- 10. SECTORS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.sectors (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    lap_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.laps(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    sector_number INTEGER NOT NULL,

    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,

    sector_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    position INTEGER,

    mini_sector_count INTEGER,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_sector_lap_number
        UNIQUE (
            lap_id,
            sector_number
        ),

    CONSTRAINT chk_sector_number
        CHECK (sector_number BETWEEN 1 AND 3)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sectors_lap
    ON public.sectors (lap_id);

-- ============================================================
-- 11. WEATHER SAMPLES
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.weather_samples (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    air_temperature_c DOUBLE PRECISION,
    track_temperature_c DOUBLE PRECISION,

    humidity_percent DOUBLE PRECISION,
    pressure_hpa DOUBLE PRECISION,

    wind_speed_kmh DOUBLE PRECISION,
    wind_direction_deg DOUBLE PRECISION,

    rainfall BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    rainfall_rate_mm_h DOUBLE PRECISION,

    cloud_cover_percent DOUBLE PRECISION,

    raw_data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_weather_session_time
    ON public.weather_samples (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 12. REPLAY RECORDINGS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.replay_recordings (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    name TEXT NOT NULL,

    status public.replay_status
        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'CREATED',

    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    ended_at TIMESTAMPTZ,

    duration_ms BIGINT,

    storage_url TEXT,
    storage_path TEXT,

    frame_count BIGINT,

    source_sequence_start BIGINT,
    source_sequence_end BIGINT,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_replay_session
    ON public.replay_recordings (race_session_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_replay_status
    ON public.replay_recordings (status);

-- ============================================================
-- 13. REPLAY BOOKMARKS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.replay_bookmarks (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    replay_recording_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.replay_recordings(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    timestamp_ms BIGINT NOT NULL,

    label TEXT NOT NULL,

    description TEXT,

    category TEXT,

    driver_id UUID REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    lap_number INTEGER,

    position_data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_replay_bookmarks_replay
    ON public.replay_bookmarks (replay_recording_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_replay_bookmarks_time
    ON public.replay_bookmarks (
        replay_recording_id,
        timestamp_ms
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 14. RACE EVENTS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.race_events (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    sequence_number BIGINT,

    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,

    severity TEXT,

    lap_number INTEGER,
    sector_number INTEGER,

    position INTEGER,

    message TEXT,

    data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_events_session_time
    ON public.race_events (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_events_type
    ON public.race_events (
        race_session_id,
        event_type
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_race_events_driver
    ON public.race_events (
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 15. CAMERA EVENTS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.camera_events (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    camera_id TEXT NOT NULL,

    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,

    driver_id UUID REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    previous_camera_id TEXT,

    shot_type TEXT,

    reason TEXT,

    score DOUBLE PRECISION,

    duration_ms BIGINT,

    source TEXT,

    data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_camera_events_session_time
    ON public.camera_events (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_camera_events_camera
    ON public.camera_events (
        camera_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_camera_events_driver
    ON public.camera_events (
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 16. STATISTICS SNAPSHOTS
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.statistics_snapshots (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    lap_number INTEGER,

    position INTEGER,

    laps_completed INTEGER,

    best_lap_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    average_lap_time_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    sector_1_best_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    sector_2_best_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    sector_3_best_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    top_speed_kmh DOUBLE PRECISION,

    average_speed_kmh DOUBLE PRECISION,

    pit_stops INTEGER,

    tyre_compound TEXT,

    tyre_age_laps INTEGER,

    gap_to_leader_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,
    gap_to_ahead_ms DOUBLE PRECISION,

    data JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_statistics_session_time
    ON public.statistics_snapshots (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_statistics_driver_time
    ON public.statistics_snapshots (
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

-- ============================================================
-- 17. DRIVER / SESSION PARTICIPATION
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.race_session_drivers (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID NOT NULL
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    car_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.cars(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    race_number INTEGER,

    grid_position INTEGER,

    finish_position INTEGER,

    status TEXT,

    points DOUBLE PRECISION DEFAULT 0,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_session_driver
        UNIQUE (
            race_session_id,
            driver_id
        )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_session_drivers_session
    ON public.race_session_drivers (race_session_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_session_drivers_driver
    ON public.race_session_drivers (driver_id);

-- ============================================================
-- 18. GENERIC TELEMETRY SOURCE REGISTRY
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.telemetry_sources (

    provider TEXT,

    endpoint TEXT,

    enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    configuration JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    last_seen_at TIMESTAMPTZ,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    CONSTRAINT uq_telemetry_source_name
        UNIQUE (name)
);

    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    driver_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    analysis_type TEXT NOT NULL,

    confidence DOUBLE PRECISION,

    title TEXT,

    summary TEXT,

    payload JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    model_name TEXT,
    model_version TEXT,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_analytics_session_time

    ON public.analytics_events (
        driver_id,
        recorded_at
    );

-- 20. COMMENTARY OUTPUT
-- ============================================================

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.commentary_events (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),

    race_session_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.race_sessions(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    recorded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,

    priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    text TEXT NOT NULL,

    driver_id UUID
        REFERENCES public.drivers(id)
        ON DELETE SET NULL,

    confidence DOUBLE PRECISION,

    source TEXT,

    model_name TEXT,

    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_commentary_session_time
    ON public.commentary_events (
        race_session_id,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_commentary_priority
    ON public.commentary_events (
        race_session_id,
        priority DESC,
        recorded_at
    );

CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW public.v_current_driver_positions AS
SELECT DISTINCT ON (ts.race_session_id, ts.driver_id)
    ts.race_session_id,
    ts.driver_id,
    d.first_name,
    d.last_name,
    d.abbreviation,
    d.permanent_number,
    t.name AS team_name,

    ts.recorded_at,
    ts.position,

    ts.x,
    ts.y,
    ts.z,

    ts.world_x,
    ts.world_y,
    ts.world_z,

    ts.speed_kmh,
    ts.heading_deg,

    ts.lap_number,
    ts.sector_number,

    ts.throttle,
    ts.brake,
    ts.drs_enabled,

    ts.tyre_compound,
    ts.tyre_age_laps

FROM public.telemetry_samples ts
JOIN public.drivers d
    ON d.id = ts.driver_id
LEFT JOIN public.teams t
    ON t.id = d.team_id

ORDER BY
    ts.race_session_id,
    ts.driver_id,
    ts.recorded_at DESC;


CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW public.v_latest_weather AS
SELECT DISTINCT ON (race_session_id)
    race_session_id,
    recorded_at,
    air_temperature_c,
    track_temperature_c,
    humidity_percent,
    pressure_hpa,
    wind_speed_kmh,
    wind_direction_deg,
    rainfall,
    rainfall_rate_mm_h,
    cloud_cover_percent
FROM public.weather_samples
ORDER BY
    race_session_id,
    recorded_at DESC;


CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW public.v_latest_statistics AS
SELECT DISTINCT ON (
    race_session_id,
    driver_id
)
    race_session_id,
    driver_id,
    recorded_at,
    lap_number,
    position,
    laps_completed,
    best_lap_time_ms,
    average_lap_time_ms,
    top_speed_kmh,
    average_speed_kmh,
    pit_stops,
    tyre_compound,
    tyre_age_laps,
    gap_to_leader_ms,
    gap_to_ahead_ms,
    data
FROM public.statistics_snapshots
ORDER BY
    race_session_id,
    driver_id,
    recorded_at DESC;

-- ============================================================
-- RLS is enabled but policies are deliberately NOT added here.
-- This prevents accidental public write access while allowing the
-- backend/service-role architecture to be established first.
--
-- The RaceVision backend should normally connect using a secure
-- server-side PostgreSQL connection or Supabase service role.
-- Public client access should be explicitly granted later.

ALTER TABLE public.teams ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.drivers ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.cars ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.tracks ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

ALTER TABLE public.replay_recordings ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.replay_bookmarks ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.race_events ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.camera_events ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.statistics_snapshots ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.race_session_drivers ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.telemetry_sources ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.analytics_events ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
ALTER TABLE public.commentary_events ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

COMMIT;
```

### 2. What this creates

The resulting database has the main RaceVision persistence structure:

```text
teams
 └── drivers
      └── cars

tracks
 └── race_sessions
      ├── race_session_drivers
      ├── telemetry_samples
      ├── laps
      │    └── sectors
      ├── weather_samples
      ├── race_events
      ├── camera_events
      ├── statistics_snapshots
      ├── analytics_events
      ├── commentary_events
      └── replay_recordings
             └── replay_bookmarks

telemetry_sources
```

That maps closely to the domain you've been building around **live F1 telemetry, persistence, replay, cameras/director, analytics and AI commentary**.

### 3. Verify the database

After running the main script, run this:

```sql
SELECT
    table_name,
    (
        SELECT COUNT(*)
        FROM information_schema.columns c
        WHERE c.table_schema = 'public'
          AND c.table_name = t.table_name
    ) AS column_count
FROM information_schema.tables t
WHERE t.table_schema = 'public'
  AND t.table_type = 'BASE TABLE'
ORDER BY t.table_name;
```

And this to confirm the important indexes:

```sql
SELECT
    schemaname,
    tablename,
    indexname
FROM pg_indexes
WHERE schemaname = 'public'
ORDER BY tablename, indexname;
```

### 4. One important RaceVision issue

There is a distinction between **a good Supabase database schema** and **a schema that exactly matches the current RaceVision SQLAlchemy models/migrations**.

Your project already has an ORM/database architecture, so I would **not blindly replace your Alembic model structure with a newly invented SQL schema**. The safest architecture is:

```text
RaceVision SQLAlchemy Models
          ↓
        Alembic
          ↓
   PostgreSQL / Supabase
          ↓
      RaceVision
```

rather than:

```text
Supabase manually modified
          ↓
RaceVision ORM
          ↓
schema mismatch
```

So the SQL above is a strong **Supabase bootstrap**, but for your actual deployment I would next reconcile it against the **current RaceVision repository's models and Alembic migrations**. That is especially important because your project has evolved substantially through Phases 10–18.

Also, your recent FastAPI Cloud failure is a **Python packaging/build problem**, not a PostgreSQL/Supabase database problem; the recorded failure was setuptools discovering multiple top-level packages in the RaceVision flat layout. fileciteturn0file5

I can also create a **RaceVision-specific Supabase database diagram** showing every table and relationship.

Some logs from Supabase;

### Entity

svgpublic.v\_current\_driver\_positions

### Issue

View **`public.v_current_driver_positions`** is defined with the SECURITY DEFINER property

### Description

Detects views defined with the SECURITY DEFINER property. These views enforce Postgres permissions and row level security policies (RLS) of the view creator, rather than that of the querying user

svgpublic.v\_latest\_weather

### Issue

View **`public.v_latest_weather`** is defined with the SECURITY DEFINER property

### Description

Detects views defined with the SECURITY DEFINER property. These views enforce Postgres permissions and row level security policies (RLS) of the view creator, rather than that of the querying user

View **`public.v_latest_statistics`** is defined with the SECURITY DEFINER property

## Assistant

Those Supabase warnings are legitimate, and they come directly from the way I wrote the three views in the earlier bootstrap script.

By default, PostgreSQL views use the **view creator's permissions**, and in Supabase that commonly means a view can bypass the RLS policies you intended on the underlying tables. Supabase specifically recommends `security_invoker = true` for views that should obey the querying user's permissions and RLS. citeturn897232search0turn897232search2

For RaceVision, I recommend fixing all three.

### Run this in Supabase SQL Editor

```sql
BEGIN;

ALTER VIEW public.v_current_driver_positions
SET (security_invoker = true);

ALTER VIEW public.v_latest_weather
SET (security_invoker = true);

ALTER VIEW public.v_latest_statistics
SET (security_invoker = true);

COMMIT;
```

That is the simplest fix and **does not require dropping/recreating the views**. PostgreSQL supports changing the `security_invoker` property with `ALTER VIEW`. citeturn897232search9

### Then verify

Run:

```sql
SELECT
    schemaname,
    viewname,
    viewowner,
    definition
FROM pg_views
WHERE schemaname = 'public'
  AND viewname IN (
      'v_current_driver_positions',
      'v_latest_weather',
      'v_latest_statistics'
  );
```

And specifically check the security setting:

```sql
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    c.relname AS view_name,
    c.reloptions
FROM pg_class c
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = c.relnamespace
WHERE c.relkind = 'v'
  AND n.nspname = 'public'
  AND c.relname IN (
      'v_current_driver_positions',
      'v_latest_weather',
      'v_latest_statistics'
  );
```

You should see:

```text
security_invoker=true
```

in `reloptions` for each view.

### Why this is particularly important for RaceVision

You have RLS enabled on the underlying RaceVision tables. With the original view definition, a caller could potentially query a view and have the underlying table access evaluated using the **view owner's privileges**, rather than the caller's RLS context. Supabase explicitly warns that views can otherwise bypass RLS. citeturn897232search2

With:

```sql
security_invoker = true
```

the effective model becomes:

```text
Supabase / API user
        │
        ▼
RaceVision View
        │
        ▼
caller permissions + caller RLS
        │
        ▼
RaceVision tables
```

rather than:

```text
Supabase / API user
        │
        ▼
RaceVision View
        │
        ▼
view owner's permissions
        │
        ▼
underlying tables
```

That is the model I would use for your public-facing RaceVision data views.

### One further security point

Changing the three views to `security_invoker` fixes the warning, but **your RLS policies themselves still need to be designed**. Enabling RLS without policies doesn't automatically make the data available to `anon` or `authenticated`; Supabase notes that grants and policies both matter. citeturn897232search2

For RaceVision, I would **not make telemetry tables publicly writable**. A sensible eventual separation is:

```text
                    ┌───────────────────────┐
                    │ RaceVision FastAPI     │
                    │ trusted backend        │
                    └───────────┬───────────┘
                                │
                         service connection
                                │
                                ▼
                    ┌───────────────────────┐
                    │ Supabase PostgreSQL    │
                    │                       │
                    │ Teams                 │
                    │ Drivers               │
                    │ Cars                  │
                    │ Tracks                │
                    │ Sessions              │
                    │ Telemetry              │
                    │ Laps / Sectors        │
                    │ Weather               │
                    │ Replay                 │
                    │ Events                 │
                    │ Camera                 │
                    │ Analytics              │
                    └───────────┬───────────┘
                                │
                         read-only views
                                │
                                ▼
                    ┌───────────────────────┐
                    │ Web / Unity / clients │
                    └───────────────────────┘
```

One other thing from your logs is worth correcting before we go much further: the **Supabase schema should ultimately match the actual RaceVision SQLAlchemy/Alembic schema**, rather than becoming a separate hand-maintained database design. Your project has evolved significantly since the original database foundation, so that reconciliation is important.

The three warnings you're seeing, however, are straightforward: **run the `ALTER VIEW ... security_invoker=true` script above and the Security Advisor warnings should be resolved for those views.**

## Assistant

Yes — these are also real security findings, and I would fix them before exposing the Supabase project to RaceVision clients.

The three findings are related but have different fixes.

### 1. `set_updated_at` — fix the mutable `search_path`

Your trigger function should explicitly pin its `search_path`. This prevents object resolution from being influenced by the caller/session search path.

Run:

```sql
ALTER FUNCTION public.set_updated_at()
SET search_path = pg_catalog, public;
```

I would actually make the function definition explicit as well:

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY INVOKER
SET search_path = pg_catalog, public
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at = NOW();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

Then verify:

```sql
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    p.prosecdef AS security_definer,
    p.proconfig AS configuration
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'public'
  AND p.proname = 'set_updated_at';
```

You want `security_definer = false` and the configuration to contain the fixed `search_path`.

---

## 2. `rls_auto_enable()` — this should NOT be publicly executable

This is the more important finding.

Supabase is telling you that:

```text
anon
   ↓
/rest/v1/rpc/rls_auto_enable
   ↓
public.rls_auto_enable()
   ↓
SECURITY DEFINER
```

and likewise for `authenticated`.

For RaceVision, there is no reason an anonymous Internet client should be able to invoke a function whose purpose is to automatically alter/enable RLS.

I recommend **revoking execution entirely** from both roles.

Run:

```sql
REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM anon;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM authenticated;
```

I would also revoke it from `PUBLIC`, because PostgreSQL functions normally grant `EXECUTE` to `PUBLIC` unless that privilege is removed:

```sql
REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM PUBLIC;
```

Then verify:

```sql
SELECT
    routine_schema,
    routine_name,
    routine_type,
    security_type
FROM information_schema.routines
WHERE routine_schema = 'public'
  AND routine_name = 'rls_auto_enable';
```

And check privileges directly:

```sql
SELECT
    routine_schema,
    routine_name,
    grantee,
    privilege_type
FROM information_schema.routine_privileges
WHERE routine_schema = 'public'
  AND routine_name = 'rls_auto_enable'
ORDER BY grantee;
```

You do **not** want `anon` or `authenticated` showing `EXECUTE`.

---

## 3. I would go one step further for RaceVision

I don't think `rls_auto_enable()` belongs in the exposed `public` API schema at all.

The RaceVision architecture already has a controlled backend boundary:

```text
Internet
   │
   ▼
RaceVision FastAPI
   │
   ├── authentication
   ├── authorization
   ├── API validation
   └── business logic
            │
            ▼
     Supabase PostgreSQL
```

The browser/Unity/OBS-facing clients should not be able to call database-administration functions directly.

So I'd prefer:

```text
PUBLIC API
    │
    ├── SELECT-safe views
    ├── controlled data endpoints
    └── RaceVision FastAPI
             │
             ▼
        PostgreSQL
             │
             └── administrative functions
                  NOT exposed through /rest/v1/rpc
```

`rls_auto_enable()` looks like something that should be used during database setup/migration, not at runtime by an end user.

---

# 4. Check whether there are any other dangerous functions

Because Supabase has already found one exposed `SECURITY DEFINER` function, I'd run this audit now:

```sql
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    p.prosecdef AS security_definer,
    p.proacl AS privileges,
    p.proconfig AS configuration
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'public'
ORDER BY p.proname;
```

Then specifically find all `SECURITY DEFINER` functions:

```sql
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    p.proconfig AS configuration
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE p.prosecdef = true
ORDER BY n.nspname, p.proname;
```

For RaceVision, I would treat every `SECURITY DEFINER` function as something that needs an explicit reason to exist.

---

# 5. Recommended combined fix

You can safely run this as one SQL Editor script:

```sql
BEGIN;

-- ============================================================
-- FIX 1: Secure search_path for updated_at trigger
-- ============================================================

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY INVOKER
SET search_path = pg_catalog, public
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at = NOW();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;

-- ============================================================
-- FIX 2: Remove public/RLS-admin RPC execution
-- ============================================================

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM PUBLIC;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM anon;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM authenticated;

COMMIT;
```

Then run:

```sql
-- Verify set_updated_at
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    p.prosecdef AS security_definer,
    p.proconfig AS configuration
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'public'
  AND p.proname = 'set_updated_at';

-- Verify rls_auto_enable
SELECT
    routine_schema,
    routine_name,
    grantee,
    privilege_type
FROM information_schema.routine_privileges
WHERE routine_schema = 'public'
  AND routine_name = 'rls_auto_enable'
ORDER BY grantee;
```

## One thing I would change from the original database script

The original script was deliberately conservative about RLS, but it has now exposed that the database was created with some extra function/security objects that weren't necessary for the core RaceVision schema.

I would **not keep adding patches one warning at a time**. At this point, the better approach is to do a complete Supabase security pass over:

**tables → RLS → views → functions → grants → exposed schemas → API access → service-role access → indexes → telemetry retention.**

That will give us a much cleaner production database for the RaceVision FastAPI deployment rather than repeatedly fixing Supabase Security Advisor warnings after the fact.

## User

This is with all tables 

### RLS Enabled No Policy

**security**

svg

### Entity

svgpublic.analytics\_events

### Issue

Table **`public.analytics_events`** has RLS enabled, but no policies exist

### Description

Detects cases where row level security (RLS) has been enabled on a table but no RLS policies have been created.

## Assistant

Yes. This warning is expected from the database script I gave you, because I enabled RLS on the tables but deliberately did not create policies.

For **RaceVision**, though, I would not simply add `USING (true)` policies to make the warning disappear. That could accidentally expose all telemetry and analytics data.

The correct design is to decide **who should read/write each class of data**.

For the specific table:

```text
public.analytics_events
```

I recommend:

```text
Internet/client
      │
      ▼
RaceVision FastAPI
      │
      │ authenticated/authorised
      ▼
Supabase PostgreSQL
      │
      └── analytics_events
```

The backend should be able to insert and read analytics, while anonymous users should not have direct database write access.

### For now, fix `analytics_events` like this

Run:

```sql
BEGIN;

-- Ensure RLS remains enabled
ALTER TABLE public.analytics_events ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;

-- Backend/service role:
-- Supabase's service_role bypasses RLS, so no policy is required
-- for normal backend service-role access.

-- Authenticated users:
-- Allow read access only when explicitly authorised.
-- Start with NO authenticated policy rather than exposing the table.

-- Anonymous users:
-- No policy = no access.

COMMIT;
```

However, **this will not remove the Supabase warning**. Supabase is warning precisely because there is no policy.

For a production RaceVision system, I suggest adding an explicit **read policy** only if authenticated clients are supposed to query analytics directly.

For example:

```sql
CREATE POLICY "authenticated_read_analytics_events"
ON public.analytics_events
FOR SELECT
TO authenticated
USING (true);
```

Then anonymous users still cannot read it, and authenticated users can.

But I would **not recommend that policy yet** until we decide whether your architecture is:

```text
Client → Supabase directly
```

or:

```text
Client → RaceVision FastAPI → Supabase
```

From the architecture you've built, the second is the better fit.

### The important point

Your current warning:

> RLS Enabled No Policy

is **not the same thing as a vulnerability**.

With RLS enabled and no policies, normal roles are denied access. The problem is that it is ambiguous/incomplete configuration, and Supabase flags it so you explicitly define the intended access model.

For RaceVision, I would create policies deliberately rather than blindly making every table public.

### I recommend we do the whole database properly

You now have warnings for:

- views using `SECURITY DEFINER`
- `set_updated_at()` mutable `search_path`
- exposed `rls_auto_enable()`
- **RLS enabled with no policies**

Since this is the database that will sit behind your RaceVision deployment, the next step should be a **complete RLS policy set for every table**, separating:

```text
PUBLIC READ
AUTHENTICATED READ
BACKEND WRITE
ADMIN WRITE
NO CLIENT ACCESS
```

That is much safer than fixing each Supabase warning individually.

I can give you one complete SQL script that applies the appropriate RLS policies to **all RaceVision tables** and is designed around your existing **FastAPI + Supabase PostgreSQL architecture**.

import os

from fastapi import FastAPI
from sqlmodel import Field, Session, SQLModel, create_engine, select


class Hero(SQLModel, table=True):
    id: int | None = Field(default=None, primary_key=True)
    name: str = Field(index=True)
    secret_name: str
    age: int | None = Field(default=None, index=True)


# The database URL is automatically injected as an environment variable
engine = create_engine(os.getenv("DATABASE_URL"))


def create_db_and_tables():

app = FastAPI()


@app.on_event("startup")
def on_startup():
    create_db_and_tables()


@app.post("/heroes/")
def create_hero(hero: Hero):
    with Session(engine) as session:
        session.add(hero)
        session.commit()
        session.refresh(hero)
        return hero


@app.get("/heroes/")

# C. Verify the actual Supabase schema

You've already confirmed:

```text
18 tables
RLS = true
0 rows
PostgreSQL 17.6
```

Now verify the columns.

Run this in Supabase SQL Editor:

```sql
SELECT
    table_name,
    ordinal_position,
    column_name,
    data_type,
    is_nullable,
    column_default
FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_schema = 'public'
ORDER BY table_name, ordinal_position;
```

Then foreign keys:

```sql
SELECT
    tc.table_name,
    kcu.column_name,
    ccu.table_name AS referenced_table,
    ccu.column_name AS referenced_column,
    tc.constraint_name
FROM information_schema.table_constraints AS tc
JOIN information_schema.key_column_usage AS kcu
    ON tc.constraint_name = kcu.constraint_name
   AND tc.table_schema = kcu.table_schema
JOIN information_schema.constraint_column_usage AS ccu
    ON ccu.constraint_name = tc.constraint_name
   AND ccu.table_schema = tc.table_schema
WHERE tc.constraint_type = 'FOREIGN KEY'
  AND tc.table_schema = 'public'
ORDER BY tc.table_name, kcu.column_name;
```

Then unique constraints:

```sql
SELECT
    tc.table_name,
    tc.constraint_name,
    tc.constraint_type
FROM information_schema.table_constraints tc
WHERE tc.table_schema = 'public'
  AND tc.constraint_type IN ('PRIMARY KEY', 'UNIQUE')
ORDER BY tc.table_name, tc.constraint_type, tc.constraint_name;
```

This matters because our earlier bootstrap schema was a **manual Supabase bootstrap**, whereas your RaceVision repository has its own established SQLAlchemy/Alembic design.

The repository should remain the authoritative application schema.

# D. Fix the Supabase security findings

Now we address the findings you've shown.

## D1. Fix the views

You already have these:

```text
public.v_current_driver_positions
public.v_latest_weather
public.v_latest_statistics
```

Run:

```sql
BEGIN;

ALTER VIEW public.v_current_driver_positions
SET (security_invoker = true);

ALTER VIEW public.v_latest_weather
SET (security_invoker = true);

ALTER VIEW public.v_latest_statistics
SET (security_invoker = true);

COMMIT;
```

PostgreSQL 15+ supports `security_invoker = true` specifically so a view uses the permissions/RLS of the querying user; Supabase recommends this for views over protected tables. citeturn388070search0turn388070search1

---

# E. Fix the trigger function

Run:

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY INVOKER
SET search_path = pg_catalog, public
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at = NOW();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

The explicit `search_path` removes the mutable-search-path finding.

---

# F. Disable the exposed `rls_auto_enable()` RPC

This should not be callable by arbitrary API users.

Run:

```sql
BEGIN;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM PUBLIC;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM anon;

REVOKE EXECUTE
ON FUNCTION public.rls_auto_enable()
FROM authenticated;

COMMIT;
```

Then inspect it:

```sql
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    p.prosecdef AS security_definer,
    p.proconfig AS configuration
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'public'
  AND p.proname = 'rls_auto_enable';
```

Supabase's security guidance specifically recommends reviewing `SECURITY DEFINER` functions and granting `EXECUTE` only to roles that actually need them. citeturn388070search4

---

# G. Now deal with the RLS "no policy" findings

This part needs more care.

You currently have:

```text
18 tables
18 RLS enabled
0 policies on at least some/all tables
```

That means direct client access is currently blocked by RLS unless a matching policy exists, but we need explicit policy design. Supabase recommends both **grants and policies**, and notes that policies alone do not remove existing table privileges. citeturn388070search0

For RaceVision, I recommend this security model:

```text
ROLE                    ACCESS
──────────────────────────────────────────
anon                    No direct DB access
authenticated           Only explicitly public/read-safe data
RaceVision backend      Read/write required application data
admin/migration         Schema administration
service_role            Backend-only emergency/full access
```

Critically:

**Do not create `USING (true)` policies across every table.**

That would defeat the purpose of your RLS architecture.

---

Before creating policies, run a grant audit.

```sql
SELECT
    grantee,
    table_schema,
    table_name,
    privilege_type
FROM information_schema.role_table_grants
WHERE table_schema = 'public'
  AND table_name IN (
      'analytics_events',
      'camera_events',
      'cars',
      'commentary_events',
      'drivers',
      'laps',
      'race_events',
      'race_session_drivers',
      'race_sessions',
      'replay_bookmarks',
      'replay_recordings',
      'sectors',
      'statistics_snapshots',
      'teams',
      'telemetry_samples',
      'telemetry_sources',
      'tracks',
      'weather_samples'
  )
ORDER BY table_name, grantee, privilege_type;
```

**Send me that result before changing the grants.**

This is important because Supabase documents that RLS policies and PostgreSQL grants are separate layers of authorization. citeturn388070search0turn388070search4

# N. Your FastAPI Cloud database connection

The FastAPI Cloud integration information you've shown is consistent with FastAPI Cloud injecting:

```text
DATABASE_URL
```

from your Supabase integration.

Supabase officially supports connection strings for server-side PostgreSQL clients, with direct, session-pooler and transaction-pooler approaches depending on the environment. citeturn388070search6

Given that your FastAPI service is a persistent application with SQLAlchemy pools, we should inspect the actual generated `DATABASE_URL` **host/port format** before deciding whether the current connection mode is optimal.

Do not paste the password or full secret URL.

Show only:

```text
scheme://username:REDACTED@HOST:PORT/database
```

# RaceVision Database Path Audit (read-only)

## Deployment path (summary)

`backend.production.asgi:app` → `create_production_app()` → `build_configuration()` → `DatabaseSettings()` → `DatabaseManager(settings=…)` → lazy `SyncEngineFactory` / `AsyncEngineFactory` → SQLAlchemy engines/pools. Health/readiness use `DatabaseHealthCheck` on that manager.

---

### 1. Exact file that reads `DATABASE_URL`

`backend/config/database.py` — class `DatabaseSettings`, field `database_url`.

Consumed when building DSNs via `DatabaseSettings.sync_dsn()` / `async_dsn()`.

Loaded into the running app via `ConfigurationService.load()` → `DatabaseSettings()` in `backend/config/service.py`, then passed in `backend/production/app.py` (`create_production_app`).

### 2. Exact environment / configuration variables

| Purpose | Env vars |
|--------|-----------|
| Full URL (preferred) | `DATABASE_URL` or `DB_URL` |
| Discrete fallback | `DB_HOST` / `POSTGRES_HOST`, `DB_PORT` / `POSTGRES_PORT`, `DB_NAME` / `POSTGRES_DB` / `DB_DATABASE`, `DB_USER` / `POSTGRES_USER` / `DB_USERNAME`, `DB_PASSWORD` / `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` |
| Pool / SSL / timeouts | `DB_POOL_SIZE`, `DB_MAX_OVERFLOW`, `DB_POOL_TIMEOUT`, `DB_POOL_RECYCLE`, `DB_SSL_MODE`, `DB_CONNECT_TIMEOUT`, etc. |

If `database_url` is set, it **overrides** discrete host/user/password for engine URL construction.

### 3. Sync engine construction

- `backend/database/manager.py` — `DatabaseManager.engine` → `_build_sync_engine()`
- `backend/database/engines.py` — `SyncEngineFactory.create()`
- Call: `sqlalchemy.create_engine(url, echo=…, echo_pool=…, connect_args=…, future=True, **pool_kwargs)`
- URL: `DatabaseSettings.sync_dsn()` → `postgresql+psycopg://…` (from URL or discrete fields)

### 4. Async engine construction

- `DatabaseManager.async_engine` → `_build_async_engine()`
- `AsyncEngineFactory.create()`
- Call: `sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio.create_async_engine(url, echo=…, echo_pool=…, connect_args=…, **pool_kwargs)`
- URL: `DatabaseSettings.async_dsn()` → `postgresql+asyncpg://…`

### 5. Connection pool configuration

From `DatabaseSettings` + `_pool_kwargs()` in `engines.py`:

| Setting | Default | Env |
|--------|---------|-----|
| `pool_size` | 5 | `DB_POOL_SIZE` |
| `max_overflow` | 10 | `DB_MAX_OVERFLOW` |
| `pool_timeout` | 30.0 | `DB_POOL_TIMEOUT` |
| `pool_recycle` | 1800 (0 → `-1` in factory) | `DB_POOL_RECYCLE` |
| `pool_pre_ping` | **True** (hardcoded in factory) | — |

Also: `connect_timeout` (default 10), `application_name` (default `racevision`).

### 6. Database health / readiness

| Layer | Location | Behavior |
|-------|----------|----------|
| Manager probes | `DatabaseManager.test_connection` / `atest_connection` | `SELECT 1` |
| Manager ping | `health_ping` / `ahealth_ping` | latency + ok/error (errors redacted) |
| Health check | `DatabaseHealthCheck.check` / `acheck` (`backend/database/health.py`) | `SELECT 1`, `SHOW server_version`, `alembic_version`, pool usage |
| HTTP readiness | `backend/api/routes/readiness.py` — `get_readiness` | Prod-like profiles require `app.state.database_healthcheck`; needs connection up + migration version present |
| HTTP liveness | `backend/api/routes/liveness.py` | **In-process only** (no DB) |
| HTTP `/health` | `backend/api/routes/health.py` | Transport/runtime health via facade — **not** a DB ping |

Production wires: `app.state.database_manager` + `app.state.database_healthcheck` in `create_production_app()`.

### 7. Safe database-backed API endpoints

In the **deployed public/runtime API**, the clear DB-backed surface is:

- `GET /api/v1/readiness` (prod-like) — uses `DatabaseHealthCheck`

Most REST/session routes (`backend/api/routes/sessions.py`, public REST) go through `RuntimeFacade` / Phase10 harness (**in-memory**), not SQLAlchemy repositories.

DB access elsewhere is infrastructure/services (manager, health, migrations, persistence adapters), not first-class public “CRUD over Postgres” REST in this API layer.

### 8. Repository SELECT example

`backend/repositories/base.py` — `BaseRepository.get()`  
→ `self._session.get(self._model, entity_id)` (PK load).

Also: `BaseRepository.list()` / `ReadRepository.count()` via `sqlalchemy.select(...)`.

Typed UoW access: `backend/repositories/unit_of_work.py` — `UnitOfWork` (e.g. `.drivers`, `.sessions`, `.telemetry`).

### 9. Repository INSERT example

`backend/repositories/base.py` — `BaseRepository.add()`  
→ `session.add(entity)` + `flush()` (no commit; UoW owns commit).

Bulk: `WriteRepository.add_many()`; async bulk Core insert: `UowTelemetryPersistAdapter.persist_rows()` in `backend/persistence/adapters.py` (`insert(TelemetrySample)`).

### 10. Is Alembic authoritative?

**Yes** for production schema.

- `backend/alembic.ini` → `database/migrations`
- Health reads `alembic_version`
- Ops: `scripts/deployment/migrations.py`, `DatabaseService.migration_status()`

### 11. `create_all()` / SQLModel?

- **SQLModel**: not used.
- `Base.metadata.create_all(engine)`: only in `backend/database/testkit.py` (SQLite/test fixtures), **not** production startup.

### 12. Direct SQL bypassing repositories/UoW?

**Yes, intentionally** in DB infrastructure:

- Health/manager: `text("SELECT 1")`, `SHOW server_version`, `alembic_version`
- `QueryExecutor` (`backend/database/executor.py`) — raw SQL helper
- `TransactionManager` — `SET LOCAL TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL …`
- `UowTelemetryPersistAdapter` — Core `insert()` (still under `AsyncUnitOfWork`)

Application repositories are the intended ORM path; health/ops use direct SQL.

### 13. Is `DATABASE_URL` logged or exposed?

**Full URL / password: no** (by design after 18.5-F).

Startup logs use `DatabaseSettings.safe_connection_summary()` → host/port/name + `database_url_configured` (boolean). Engine create logs dialect/pool size only. Health errors go through `redact_postgres_credentials()`. Failures name config keys (`DATABASE_URL`), not values.

### 14. Dev vs production DB configuration discrepancy

| | Development | Production-like (`backend.production`) |
|--|-------------|----------------------------------------|
| Profile default | `resolve_profile` → `development` if unset | Entrypoint defaults to **`production`** if unset |
| DB defaults | `localhost:5432`, user/pass `racevision` allowed | Refuses localhost without `DATABASE_URL`; refuses default password without URL |
| YAML | `config/development/settings.yaml` (DEBUG etc.; no DB URL) | `config/production/settings.yaml` (logging/TLS flags; secrets from env) |
| API DB readiness | Often skipped if profile not prod-like | Required via `/readiness` |
| Schema | Testkit may `create_all` on SQLite | Alembic only |

**Note:** Platform env `PORT` (HTTP) must not be treated as `DB_PORT`; filtered in `DatabaseSettings` settings sources so Cloud `PORT=8080` does not become the DB port.

You are working on the RaceVision repository.

## OBJECTIVE

Make the deployed FastAPI production application explicitly use the
production configuration/profile so that:

1. FastAPI Cloud uses the production profile.
2. DATABASE\_URL remains the authoritative database connection setting.
3. Production readiness requires and verifies the PostgreSQL database.
4. Existing RaceVision SQLAlchemy / DatabaseManager / Alembic architecture
   is preserved.
5. No SQLModel is introduced.
6. No create\_all() is introduced into production.
7. No Runtime/EventBus/transport architecture is redesigned.
8. No secrets are exposed or committed.
9. All relevant tests, linting and type checks pass.
10. Commit the completed change to GitHub.

## IMPORTANT ARCHITECTURE

Existing verified deployment path:

backend.production.asgi\:app
→ create\_production\_app()
→ build\_configuration()
→ DatabaseSettings()
→ DatabaseManager(settings=…)
→ SyncEngineFactory / AsyncEngineFactory
→ SQLAlchemy
→ Supabase PostgreSQL

Verified database architecture:

- DATABASE\_URL / DB\_URL is the preferred database configuration.
- DATABASE\_URL overrides discrete DB\_HOST/DB\_PORT/DB\_NAME/etc.
- SQLAlchemy sync + async engines are used.
- pool\_pre\_ping is enabled.
- DatabaseHealthCheck performs real DB checks including SELECT 1,
  server version and Alembic migration state.
- Alembic is the authoritative production schema mechanism.
- SQLModel is NOT used.
- Base.metadata.create\_all() is test-only.
- Platform HTTP PORT=8080 must NOT be interpreted as DB\_PORT.

## User

Auditing profile resolution from the production ASGI entrypoint—no file changes until the root cause is confirmed in source.

## STATUS

**Audit complete. No repository changes. No commit.** Deployed process is selecting development because a profile-related env var is set to a development alias (most likely platform `ENVIRONMENT=development`). Code path is correct.

## ROOT CAUSE

`backend.production.asgi:app` calls `create_production_app()` → `resolve_production_entrypoint_profile()`.

That function defaults to **production only when none** of `RV_PROFILE` / `APP_PROFILE` / `ENVIRONMENT` are set. If any of those is present, it delegates to `resolve_profile()`, which honors the value.

Live logs (`config/development/settings.yaml`, `profile=development`, `auth_enabled=False`, `auth_provider=development`) match resolution to **development**, which only happens when one of those vars is set to a development alias (`development` / `dev` / `local`). FastAPI Cloud commonly injects `ENVIRONMENT=development`; with `RV_PROFILE` unset, that wins.

Proven in-process:

| **EnvironResolved profile**                         |               |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------------- |
| `{}`                                                | `production`  |
| `ENVIRONMENT=development`                           | `development` |
| `RV_PROFILE=production` + `ENVIRONMENT=development` | `production`  |
| `PORT=8080` only                                    | `production`  |

## PROFILE VARIABLE

Primary (highest precedence among env vars): **`RV_PROFILE`**

Also accepted: `APP_PROFILE`, then `ENVIRONMENT`

(`APP_ENV` / bare `ENV` are **not** used for profile selection.)

## REQUIRED PRODUCTION VALUE

RV\_PROFILE=production

(Aliases also accepted: `prod`. Canonical value: `production`.)

## PRECEDENCE

**Profile selection** (`resolve_profile` / production entrypoint):

1. Explicit argument (production entrypoint passes its resolved profile into `build_configuration`)
2. `RV_PROFILE`
3. `APP_PROFILE`
4. `ENVIRONMENT`
5. Default: generic `resolve_profile` → `development`; production entrypoint with **no** profile env → `production`

**Settings values** (`ConfigurationService.load`):

1. Code defaults
2. Profile YAML `config/{profile}/settings.yaml`
3. Dotenv (pre-set process env wins over dotenv)
4. Process environment
5. Runtime overrides / secrets

`PORT` does not participate in profile selection.

## WHY CURRENT DEPLOYMENT IS DEVELOPMENT

At least one of `RV_PROFILE` / `APP_PROFILE` / `ENVIRONMENT` is set to a development alias in the Cloud process environment. With `RV_PROFILE` unset, platform `ENVIRONMENT=development` alone forces development → loads `/app/config/development/settings.yaml` → AuthSettings stay development (`enabled=False`, `provider=development`). Entrypoint module name does not hard-force production when those vars are present.

## DATABASE READINESS

- **development:** readiness can return `ready=true`, `reason=runtime_wired`, `database.required=false` (DB not gated).
- **production** (and staging/cloud/kubernetes/docker): readiness requires `DatabaseHealthCheck`; no `runtime_wired` shortcut.

Architecture intact: `DATABASE_URL` authoritative; `PORT` ≠ `DB_PORT`; `DatabaseManager` + sync/async engines; Alembic authority; no SQLModel / no production `create_all()`.

## CODE CHANGE REQUIRED

**NO**

## IMPLEMENTATION

No repository change required. Application already implements the correct contract; fix is FastAPI Cloud environment configuration.

## FILES CHANGED

*(none)*

## TESTS

Not re-run for a no-change audit. Existing coverage already proves this (including `RV_PROFILE=production` overriding `ENVIRONMENT=development`, and prod-like readiness requiring DB). Last known full suite on this branch: pass (Agent 18.6).

## RUFF

N/A (no changes)

## MYPY

N/A (no changes)

## GIT COMMIT

**Not created** — deployment-only environment change.

## GIT PUSH

**Not performed** (no commit). Branch: `main` @ `76a24ee`

## FASTAPI CLOUD ACTION REQUIRED

RV\_PROFILE=production

Do not rely on `ENVIRONMENT` alone if the platform sets `ENVIRONMENT=development`. Keep Cloud `DATABASE_URL`; do not map HTTP `PORT` to `DB_PORT`.

## NEXT DEPLOYMENT VERIFICATION

After setting `RV_PROFILE=production` and redeploying, startup should show:

- `profile_yaml_loaded path=.../config/production/settings.yaml`
- `configuration_loaded ... profile=production`
- `api_startup` with production-like auth (not `auth_enabled=False` / `auth_provider=development` unless insecure override is intentionally set)

Readiness (`GET /api/v1/readiness`) should show `database.required: true` and must **not** use `reason: "runtime_wired"`.

## Assistant

These logs are very clear: **this deployment is still running `development`**.

The evidence is:

```text
profile_yaml_loaded path=/app/config/development/settings.yaml
configuration_loaded ... profile=development
```

and:

```text
auth_enabled=False
auth_provider=development
```

So **`RV_PROFILE=production` has not taken effect in this deployment**.

Also, the deployment ID is:

```text
63bf8271-50e3-4bad-b906-d9691db9e18b
```

and all the startup events you pasted are from **18:52 on September 7, 2026**. The later requests at 19:06 and 19:11 are merely requests hitting that same running deployment; they don't show a new deployment after changing the environment variable.

### What to do now

In FastAPI Cloud go to:

**RaceVision → Environment Variables**

Make sure you have:

```text
Name:  RV_PROFILE
Value: production
```

Then **save the environment variable and explicitly redeploy the application**.

Do not just refresh the logs. We need a **new deployment ID**.

### After redeployment

Look at the very beginning of the new logs.

We need to see:

```text
profile_yaml_loaded path=/app/config/production/settings.yaml
```

and:

```text
configuration_loaded ... profile=production
```

You should **not** see:

```text
config/development/settings.yaml
profile=development
```

Then check:

```text
https://racevision.fastapicloud.dev/api/v1/readiness
```

The important result is that:

```json
"database": {
    "required": true
}
```

and the reason should no longer be:

```json
"reason": "runtime_wired"
```

### One more important check

Because Cursor proved the precedence is:

```text
RV_PROFILE
    ↓
APP_PROFILE
    ↓
ENVIRONMENT
```

there is a possibility FastAPI Cloud already has an `RV_PROFILE` or `APP_PROFILE` variable with an unexpected value.

In the FastAPI Cloud Environment Variables page, check whether you have any of these:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

The desired state is:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
```

If `APP_PROFILE` or `ENVIRONMENT` exists, **don't delete them yet**. `RV_PROFILE` should override them according to Cursor's source audit.

### Don't change anything in Supabase

At this point:

```text
Supabase PostgreSQL      ✅
DATABASE_URL             ✅
RaceVision DB layer      ✅
Alembic architecture     ✅
Profile selection        ❌ still development
```

So the next action is simply **set/save `RV_PROFILE=production` and redeploy**.

After that, paste the **new deployment's first ~30 startup log lines** and the new `/api/v1/readiness` JSON. I can then confirm whether we've crossed the production-profile gate.

## Assistant

This explains exactly what happened, and **the production profile is now being selected correctly**.

The failure is the next safety gate doing exactly what it was designed to do.

### What changed

Your new deployment shows:

```text
profile_yaml_loaded path=/app/config/production/settings.yaml
```

and:

```text
configuration_loaded ... profile=production
```

So this part is now **fixed**.

The application then reaches:

```text
_validate_production_safety(settings)
```

and deliberately stops with:

```text
RuntimeError: Refusing to start: SECRET_KEY is still the insecure default
```

That is good behavior for a production application. The code is refusing to run with the known placeholder:

```text
change-me-in-production
```

So **do not modify the code to bypass this check**.

## What you need to do in FastAPI Cloud

Go back to:

**FastAPI Cloud → RaceVision → Environment Variables**

You now need to configure a real production `SECRET_KEY`.

You should have something like:

```text
SECRET_KEY = <strong random secret>
```

Do **not** paste the secret here.

A good production secret should be a long, randomly generated value, not a password you invented.

### Generate one locally

On Windows PowerShell:

```powershell
python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(64))"
```

This will produce a strong random secret.

Copy that value into:

```text
SECRET_KEY
```

in FastAPI Cloud.

### Also keep

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
```

and:

```text
DATABASE_URL
```

Do not replace the Supabase `DATABASE_URL`.

---

# Important: there may be another production safety check immediately afterwards

Your traceback shows:

```python
if settings.security.secret_key.get_secret_value() == "change-me-in-production":
    raise RuntimeError(...)
```

and then the code also checks the default database password:

```python
db_password_is_default = (
    settings.database.password.get_secret_value() == "racevision"
)
```

So after fixing `SECRET_KEY`, the application may either:

1. start successfully, or
2. stop at the next production safety requirement.

That would be normal.
