## Assistant

We found the next failure: the production profile logic is now being reached, but a **secret-looking value has been placed in a profile environment variable**. Because that value appears in the logs, treat it as exposed and rotate it.

## Assistant

We have found the actual problem.

### What happened

This error is decisive:

```text
ValueError: Unknown environment profile
'SbfVMf7O9MBrZAg5ZHg954soCcEwO3Tu5QzAkv10RlRlqiOoPXMfwHtVfZ6KnMnyHjtFyWTm1CzJZ7zi0fJp_Q'
```

That long random value is **not a valid RaceVision profile**. The application only accepts:

```text
cloud
development
docker
kubernetes
production
staging
testing
```

So one of the profile variables:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

has been given that random secret value.

Given that the value looks exactly like a generated secret, the most likely mistake is that the secret intended for `SECRET_KEY` was entered into the wrong FastAPI Cloud environment-variable field.

### Important security action

Because the secret value has now appeared in deployment logs, **do not continue using that exact secret as your `SECRET_KEY`**. Supabase's security guidance explicitly recommends rotating credentials that have been exposed, and secrets should not be placed in logs. citeturn370042search0turn370042search2

If that random value was your new `SECRET_KEY`:

1. Generate a **new** `SECRET_KEY`.
2. Put the new value only in the `SECRET_KEY` variable.
3. Make sure it is not present in `RV_PROFILE`, `APP_PROFILE`, or `ENVIRONMENT`.
4. Redeploy.

Do **not** paste the new secret here.

---

## Fix the FastAPI Cloud variables

Go to:

**FastAPI Cloud → RaceVision → Environment Variables**

You should have, conceptually:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
SECRET_KEY=<NEW RANDOM SECRET>
DATABASE_URL=<automatically supplied Supabase value>
```

Check these three profile variables:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

The required profile result is:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
```

You can leave `ENVIRONMENT` alone if FastAPI Cloud provides it, because `RV_PROFILE` has higher precedence according to the Cursor audit.

### Very important

Do **not** put the generated `SECRET_KEY` into:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

Those variables contain **profile names**, not secrets.

---

## Then redeploy

The current deployment:

```text
deployment_id=dc2f2100-b138-4ba5-bcea-8820567de44a
```

is failing before the application can start.

After correcting the variables and redeploying, the desired sequence is:

```text
profile_yaml_loaded
.../config/production/settings.yaml

configuration_loaded
profile=production
```

Then the application should proceed to the next production safety check.

### You should then see

```text
profile=production
```

and, assuming the new secret is valid, you should get past:

```text
Refusing to start: SECRET_KEY is still the insecure default
```

The application may then perform another safety check. That's expected.

---

## Don't change the code

This is **not a RaceVision code defect**.

The code is doing the right thing:

```text
invalid profile value
        ↓
reject startup
```

and earlier:

```text
production + insecure SECRET_KEY
        ↓
reject startup
```

Those are exactly the kinds of fail-closed safeguards we want in the production deployment.

Your FastAPI environment variables are the problem.

---

## After redeploy

Send me only these two things:

```text
1. The new startup log from:
   profile_yaml_loaded
   through
   Application startup complete
   (or the next startup error)

2. The new /api/v1/readiness response
```

**Do not include any secret-looking values in the logs.**

Once the service starts successfully in production, we'll verify the database readiness and then move on to the Supabase RLS/security work. FastAPI's configuration model is designed to take sensitive settings such as secret keys and database credentials from environment variables rather than source code. citeturn370042search3turn370042search10

## User

Auditing production safety validation from source only—no file changes.

## PRODUCTION SAFETY CHECKS

Startup path: `create_production_app()` → `build_configuration()` → **`_validate_production_safety(settings)`** → **`_build_auth_settings()`**** / ****`validate_auth_settings()`** → `create_app()` (CORS / rate-limit / hardening).

### A. `_validate_production_safety` (prod-like profiles only)

| **#CheckFails whenEnv that controls itSafe requirement** |                                 |                                                                         |                                                               |                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1                                                        | Debug off                       | `settings.application.debug is True`                                    | `DEBUG`                                                       | Must be `false` (or unset; production YAML sets `false`) |
| 2                                                        | Secret key not default          | `SECRET_KEY` equals `change-me-in-production`                           | `SECRET_KEY`                                                  | **Mandatory** strong non-default secret                  |
| 3                                                        | DB not localhost without URL    | No `DATABASE_URL`/`DB_URL` **and** `DB_HOST` is localhost/127.0.0.1/::1 | `DATABASE_URL` (preferred) or non-local `DB_HOST`             | **Mandatory** one of these                               |
| 4                                                        | Default DB password without URL | Discrete password is `racevision` **and** no `DATABASE_URL`             | `DB_PASSWORD` / `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, or supply `DATABASE_URL` | **Mandatory** unless URL present                         |

### B. Auth (`_build_auth_settings` → `validate_auth_settings`) — also startup-fatal

| **#CheckEnvSafe requirement** |                          |                                          |                                                                       |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 5                             | Auth enabled             | `AUTH_ENABLED`                           | Must be `true` (unless emergency `AUTH_ALLOW_INSECURE_OVERRIDE=true`) |
| 6                             | Not development provider | `AUTH_PROVIDER`                          | Must be `jwt` in production (not `development`)                       |
| 7                             | JWT issuer + audience    | `AUTH_ISSUER`, `AUTH_AUDIENCE`           | Both required when provider=`jwt`                                     |
| 8                             | JWT key material         | `AUTH_JWKS_URL` **or** `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` | At least one required                                                 |
| 9                             | Algorithms               | `AUTH_ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS`                | Optional; default `RS256,ES256,HS256` is OK                           |

### C. Later in `create_app` (can also abort startup)

| **#CheckEnvNotes** |                                      |                    |                                                                             |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------ | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 10                 | CORS `*` forbidden in prod-like auth | `API_CORS_ORIGINS` | Empty/unset = CORS disabled (OK). `*` with production auth → `RuntimeError` |

`ConfigurationService._validate_cross_cutting` only **warns** on bad `SECRET_KEY`/`DEBUG`; production entrypoint **raises**.

---

## REQUIRED ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES

RV\_PROFILE=production

SECRET\_KEY=\<strong random secret; must NOT be change-me-in-production>

DATABASE\_URL=\<Supabase/Postgres DSN; preferred over discrete DB\_\* >

AUTH\_ENABLED=true

AUTH\_PROVIDER=jwt

AUTH\_ISSUER=\<JWT issuer URL for your IdP / Supabase>

AUTH\_AUDIENCE=\<JWT audience claim expected by the API>

AUTH\_JWKS\_URL=\<JWKS URL>   # OR AUTH\_JWT\_SECRET=\<HMAC secret> instead of JWKS

DEBUG=false

If you do **not** use `DATABASE_URL`:

DB\_HOST=\<non-localhost Postgres host>

DB\_PASSWORD=\<non-default password; not racevision>

(`DB_PASSWORD` alias: `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`)

---

## OPTIONAL VARIABLES

APP\_PROFILE / ENVIRONMENT=\<profile; RV\_PROFILE wins>

DB\_URL=\<alias of DATABASE\_URL>

DB\_PORT / DB\_NAME / DB\_USER / DB\_SSL\_MODE=\<discrete DB; ignored for DSN when DATABASE\_URL set>

API\_AUTH\_TOKEN=\<optional API token slot>

AUTH\_JWT\_SECRET=\<use instead of or in addition to JWKS path; JWKS preferred for RS/ES>

AUTH\_REQUIRE\_HTTPS=true

AUTH\_ALLOWED\_ALGORITHMS=RS256,ES256,HS256

AUTH\_CLOCK\_SKEW\_SECONDS=60

AUTH\_ALLOW\_INSECURE\_OVERRIDE=true   # emergency only; not recommended

API\_CORS\_ORIGINS=\<explicit origin list; do not use \*>

API\_DOCS\_ENABLED=false              # also set by production YAML

LOG\_LEVEL / LOG\_JSON                # YAML sets INFO / true

SECURITY\_TLS\_REQUIRED=true          # YAML sets true

WEBSOCKET\_AUTH\_REQUIRED=true        # YAML sets true; also forced if AUTH\_ENABLED

`PORT` (HTTP listen) is **not** a DB setting and does **not** affect these safety checks.

---

## AUTHENTICATION REQUIREMENTS

1. **`AUTH_ENABLED`**** must be ****`true`** for production-like profiles (startup fails otherwise).
2. **Appropriate provider: ****`jwt`**. `development` is refused in production.
3. For JWT you need:
   - `AUTH_ISSUER`
   - `AUTH_AUDIENCE`
   - **`AUTH_JWKS_URL`**** or ****`AUTH_JWT_SECRET`**
4. AuthSettings are built with `environment=<resolved profile>` (so production profile drives fail-closed rules even if other env noise exists for that field).
5. Do not set `API_CORS_ORIGINS=*`.

---

## DATABASE REQUIREMENTS

1. **`DATABASE_URL`**** is authoritative** (`DATABASE_URL` / `DB_URL`); when set it drives the DSN and **bypasses** the default-password check (`racevision` is OK as unused discrete default when URL is configured).
2. Without URL: non-localhost `DB_HOST` **and** non-default `DB_PASSWORD`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD`.
3. Platform HTTP `PORT` ≠ `DB_PORT` (already filtered in `DatabaseSettings`).
4. Engine/manager/health/Alembic architecture unchanged; readiness still requires DB health in production after the app starts.

---

## YAML vs ENVIRONMENT

From `config/production/settings.yaml` (non-secret overlay; applied only if process env key is **absent**):

| **YAML keyEffect**                   |                                        |
| ------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------- |
| `DEBUG: false`                       | Satisfies debug check if `DEBUG` unset |
| `LOG_LEVEL` / `LOG_JSON`             | Logging                                |
| `API_DOCS_ENABLED: false`            | Docs off                               |
| `SECURITY_TLS_REQUIRED: true`        | TLS flag                               |
| `FEATURE_ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL: false` | Feature flag                           |
| `WEBSOCKET_AUTH_REQUIRED: true`      | WS auth                                |

**Not** in production YAML (must come from FastAPI Cloud / secrets):

- `SECRET_KEY` ← **your current failure**

and put exactly:

```text
production
```

into:

Do not change your Supabase `DATABASE_URL`.

### The important clue

Your source is explicitly evaluating:


against:

```text
change-me-in-production
```

Therefore this is **not an application-code bug**. The production configuration loader is doing exactly what it was designed to do: fail closed.

```text

APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

It must **not**.

### Fix FastAPI Cloud now

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
SECRET_KEY
```

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
SECRET_KEY=<NEW random secret>
```

For `APP_PROFILE` and `ENVIRONMENT`, preferably **remove them** unless you have a specific reason to use them. `RV_PROFILE` takes precedence.

Keep the secret completely private.

## Assistant

Yes. I reviewed the current `main` branch of `kanedark316/RaceVision`, including the profile resolver, configuration service, production app, production YAML, authentication configuration, and existing profile tests. The repository is private, but the connected GitHub integration has access. fileciteturn5file0

There are **two separate issues**, and the distinction is important.

### 1. The FastAPI Cloud environment is still misconfigured

The repository deliberately resolves the profile from:

`explicit → RV_PROFILE → APP_PROFILE → ENVIRONMENT → development`

and rejects unknown values. fileciteturn6file0

Your deployment is putting a random secret-like value into that profile-selection path. That is still a **deployment environment-variable problem**, not something we should "fix" by making profile validation more permissive.

### 2. I found a real source-code security defect

The current `resolve_profile()` does this:

```python
raise ValueError(
    f"Unknown environment profile '{raw}'. Supported: {supported}"
)
```

So when a secret accidentally lands in `RV_PROFILE`, `APP_PROFILE`, or `ENVIRONMENT`, the complete secret is copied into the exception and therefore into the deployment log. fileciteturn6file0

That's exactly what has happened to you twice.

This **should be fixed in the repository**.

The existing test suite already has `tests/config/test_profiles_and_dotenv.py` dedicated to this area, including an unknown-profile test, so this is the appropriate place to add a regression test rather than creating a new unrelated subsystem. fileciteturn17file0 fileciteturn18file0

The production application itself is correctly fail-closed on the insecure `SECRET_KEY`, database defaults, and production-like profiles. fileciteturn10file0

The production YAML also correctly contains non-secret settings only; it does not put `SECRET_KEY` into source control. fileciteturn11file0

Authentication is also correctly designed to fail closed in production: `AUTH_ENABLED`, JWT provider, issuer/audience, and JWKS/JWT secret are required as appropriate. fileciteturn14file0

## Recommended source change

I recommend **one tightly scoped security hardening change**:

`backend/config/profiles.py`

Change the unknown-profile error so it **never echoes the raw supplied value**.

For example, the error should become something like:

```text
Unknown environment profile. Supported: cloud, development, docker, kubernetes, production, staging, testing
```

Then add regression coverage proving that a secret-shaped/secret-like value is **not present in the exception message**.

I would also have the agent check whether any other configuration error paths echo raw secret-bearing environment values.

RACEVISION — PRODUCTION PROFILE ERROR SECRET-LEAK HARDENING

OBJECTIVE
---------

The current implementation in:

backend/config/profiles.py

raises an exception containing the complete invalid profile value:

    raise ValueError(f"Unknown environment profile '{raw}'. Supported: {supported}")

In FastAPI Cloud, a SECRET_KEY was accidentally assigned to a profile environment variable and the complete secret was therefore emitted into deployment logs.

The profile validation itself is CORRECT and MUST remain fail-closed.

The defect is that an invalid profile value is echoed into an exception message.

PRIMARY REQUIREMENT
-------------------

Modify the profile-resolution error handling so that an invalid profile value is NEVER included in the exception message or normal logging.

The error must identify the problem without exposing the supplied value.

Example acceptable form:

    Unknown environment profile. Supported: cloud, development, docker, kubernetes, production, staging, testing

Do NOT include:
- the raw profile
- SECRET_KEY
- DATABASE_URL
- passwords
- JWT secrets
- API tokens
- Supabase credentials
- arbitrary environment-variable values

ARCHITECTURE REQUIREMENTS
-------------------------

The precedence must remain:

2. RV_PROFILE
3. APP_PROFILE
4. ENVIRONMENT
5. development default

Preserve all existing aliases:

dev
development
local
test
testing
prod
production
staging
docker
cloud
k8s
kubernetes

Do NOT:
- accept arbitrary profiles
- silently fall back on invalid profiles
- convert invalid profiles to production
- convert invalid profiles to development
- weaken fail-closed behaviour
- remove profile validation
- change production safety checks
- change authentication requirements
- change DATABASE_URL behaviour

SECONDARY SECURITY AUDIT
------------------------

Search the configuration/profile/startup code for other exception or logging paths that may echo raw secret-bearing environment values.

Prioritize:

backend/config/

Search for:

- passwords in errors/logs

tests/config/test_profiles_and_dotenv.py

Add regression coverage proving:

1. Unknown profile still raises ValueError.

4. A secret-shaped value used as the invalid profile is NOT exposed in the exception.
5. Existing profile aliases and precedence remain unchanged.

Also inspect whether production-entrypoint profile tests should receive a regression case.

Run the focused configuration/profile tests first.

Then run the relevant production/configuration/auth test suites.

Run:
- pytest
- Ruff
- mypy

Use the repository's existing commands/configuration rather than inventing a new toolchain.

SECURITY
--------

NEVER print any real secret during testing.

Use synthetic test values only.

Do not add secrets to:
- source files
- fixtures

- test output
- documentation
- git history

Do not inspect, print, or modify external deployment secrets.

GIT SAFETY
----------

Before editing:
- inspect git status
- inspect current branch
- inspect recent relevant commits

Do not use:
- git reset --hard
- force push
- destructive branch operations

Keep the change narrowly scoped.

EXPECTED FILES
--------------

Likely:

Only modify additional files if the audit finds a directly related genuine defect.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
-------------------

The change is accepted only when:

- invalid profile values are still rejected
- invalid profile values are never echoed
- synthetic secret-shaped values are never present in profile errors
- all existing profile tests pass
- new regression tests pass

FINAL REPORT
------------

------

    security: prevent profile value leakage in errors

Then push the current branch to GitHub.

Never force push.

Do not commit any environment secrets.
Do not include deployment secret values in the commit message or report.

### Why I want this committed

This isn't merely cosmetic.

Your current production code is behaving correctly by refusing the invalid profile. The problem is that the failure path itself can disclose credentials. The source code demonstrates that directly. fileciteturn6file0

So I recommend:

**Fix the repository → test → commit → push → then fix FastAPI Cloud environment variables.**

After that, your FastAPI Cloud variables should be:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
SECRET_KEY=<fresh new secret>
AUTH_ENABLED=true
AUTH_PROVIDER=jwt
AUTH_ISSUER=<correct issuer>
AUTH_AUDIENCE=<correct audience>
AUTH_JWKS_URL=<correct JWKS URL>
DATABASE_URL=<Supabase-managed>
DEBUG=false
```

And remove `APP_PROFILE` / `ENVIRONMENT` unless they are genuinely required.

One more thing: the repository's `ConfigurationService` does correctly treat pre-existing process environment values as protected when applying dotenv/profile YAML overlays, which is good and should be preserved. fileciteturn7file0

**I would make the security hardening commit first, then redeploy with a fresh `SECRET_KEY`.**

## Assistant

This log is actually a **positive sign** compared with the earlier failures.

The important change is that the secret is **no longer appearing in the error message**. Your deployed code now contains the hardened form:

```text
ValueError: Unknown environment profile. Supported: ...
```

rather than echoing the supplied value. That means the security fix to `backend/config/profiles.py` has made it into the deployment.

However, the deployment is **still not proven healthy**. The log shows the application being started again at 23:02, but the excerpt ends immediately after:

```text
Starting FastAPI in production mode
Using import string: backend.production.asgi:app
Server started at https://racevision.fastapicloud.dev
```

So we need to establish whether the imported ASGI app actually completed startup after that point.

### What the log tells us

The sequence is now:

```text
FastAPI Cloud
    ↓
backend.production.asgi:app
    ↓
create_production_app()
    ↓
resolve_production_entrypoint_profile()
    ↓
resolve_profile()
```

The previous secret-leaking exception has been fixed, because the exception now contains only the supported profile list.

But the **underlying profile-variable problem may still exist**. The sanitized error proves that one of the profile variables is still invalid; it does not prove that FastAPI Cloud is configured correctly.

## Do this next

In FastAPI Cloud, check these variables:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
SECRET_KEY
```

The intended configuration is:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
SECRET_KEY=<fresh secret>
```

and preferably:

```text
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

should be removed unless you deliberately need them.

Do **not** put the secret into any of those profile variables.

Also, because the secret has previously appeared in logs, use a **freshly generated** `SECRET_KEY`.

---

### My assessment right now

I **do not** recommend changing `resolve_profile()` to automatically treat an invalid value as `production`. That would hide the deployment mistake and undermine one of the most important safety properties of the service.

The correct architecture is:

```text
FastAPI Cloud
   │
   ├── RV_PROFILE=production
   ├── SECRET_KEY=<secret>
   ├── AUTH_ENABLED=true
   ├── AUTH_PROVIDER=jwt
   └── DATABASE_URL=<Supabase>
            │
            ▼
backend.production.asgi:app
            │
            ▼
production profile
            │
            ▼
production safety validation
            │
            ▼
JWT validation
            │
            ▼
database startup/readiness
```

One other observation from the log: FastAPI Cloud is running **Python 3.14.7**. That isn't the cause of this particular failure, but given your repository's Python 3.11 target, I would have Cursor audit Python-version compatibility before declaring production fully accepted.

The next useful evidence is the **first log message after `Server started at https://racevision.fastapicloud.dev`**. That will tell us whether the application has finally passed profile resolution and moved on to `SECRET_KEY`, JWT, or database validation.

Yes. I reviewed the current RaceVision repository, the deployment log you uploaded, and the current Supabase authentication guidance. The repository already has the correct production entrypoint and fail-closed security model; the main problem is now making the repository and deployment configuration robust enough that FastAPI Cloud cannot accidentally turn a secret into a profile, while also making the Supabase JWT configuration explicit.

The current repository confirms:

- `backend/production/asgi.py` is the production ASGI entrypoint.
- `resolve_profile()` uses `explicit → RV_PROFILE → APP_PROFILE → ENVIRONMENT → development`.
- `backend/config/service.py` protects existing process environment values when applying dotenv/profile YAML overlays.
- `config/production/settings.yaml` contains only non-secret production settings.
- `.env.production.example` already declares `RV_PROFILE=production`, JWT settings, `DATABASE_URL`, and `SECRET_KEY` as deployment-supplied values.
- The profile error has already been hardened so it no longer echoes the invalid value. fileciteturn6file0 fileciteturn7file0 fileciteturn11file0 fileciteturn23file0
- The repository's current HEAD includes a security hardening commit immediately before the latest deployment activity. fileciteturn5file0
- Supabase's current documentation recommends the project's JWKS endpoint at `https://<project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`; Supabase JWTs use an issuer based on the project Auth URL and an audience such as `authenticated`. citeturn950923search0turn950923search3turn950923search5

The uploaded FastAPI Cloud log shows the application repeatedly reaching `backend.production.asgi:app` and then failing during profile resolution, while the sanitized profile exception now avoids leaking the supplied value. fileciteturn20file0

## Then set FastAPI Cloud like this

The source fix and the deployment settings are two different things. Cursor can fix and push the repository, but **your actual secret values belong in FastAPI Cloud**, not GitHub.

Use this exact variable structure:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production

DEBUG=false

SECRET_KEY=<fresh random secret>

AUTH_ENABLED=true
AUTH_PROVIDER=jwt
AUTH_ISSUER=https://<your-project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1
AUTH_AUDIENCE=authenticated
AUTH_JWKS_URL=https://<your-project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json
```

Supabase's current documentation supports the JWKS URL above, and current JWT guidance identifies `iss` as the issuer and commonly uses `authenticated` as the access-token audience. citeturn950923search0turn950923search3turn950923search5

Your database should remain:

```text
DATABASE_URL=<Supabase/FastAPI Cloud integration>
```

Do **not** put the Supabase secret key into `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` merely because it is available. For asymmetric Supabase signing, JWKS is the better fit, because your application verifies against public signing keys rather than needing the private signing secret. citeturn950923search0turn950923search4

## One thing I specifically want Cursor to fix

There is one repository detail I would not leave unexamined: the production app currently suppresses exceptions from `DatabaseManager.start(validate=False)`. That can be acceptable for deferred readiness, but only if `/api/v1/readiness` reliably reports the database as unavailable. The full prompt above explicitly makes Cursor audit this rather than blindly changing it. The supplied logs already show readiness requests returning HTTP 200 from another replica, so this distinction matters. fileciteturn20file0

The finished architecture should be:

# What is actually wrong

One of these FastAPI Cloud variables is still being populated with something that is **not a valid profile**:

```text
RV_PROFILE
APP_PROFILE
ENVIRONMENT
```

Your production deployment should have:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
```

and it should **not** have the secret placed in any profile variable.

Because `RV_PROFILE` has highest priority, the cleanest configuration is:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
```

Remove `APP_PROFILE` and `ENVIRONMENT` unless you specifically need them.

## FastAPI Cloud — exact configuration

Set:

```text
RV_PROFILE=production
DEBUG=false
AUTH_ENABLED=true
AUTH_PROVIDER=jwt
AUTH_ISSUER=https://<YOUR-SUPABASE-PROJECT-REF>.supabase.co/auth/v1
AUTH_AUDIENCE=authenticated

SECRET_KEY=<FRESH-SECRET>
```

Your Supabase integration should provide:

# There is one more thing I would change in the repository

The source is now secure, but the error does not tell you **which profile environment variable name** is wrong.

That is why you're having to infer whether it is `RV_PROFILE`, `APP_PROFILE`, or `ENVIRONMENT`.

A better production diagnostic would say something like:

```text
Invalid environment profile configuration in RV_PROFILE.
Supported profiles: cloud, development, docker, kubernetes, production, staging, testing
```

without ever printing the value.

That is a legitimate quality/security improvement because it gives the operator actionable information without leaking the secret.

However, **I would not make this your next change yet**. First correct FastAPI Cloud. Otherwise we'll just create another deployment while the actual environment variable remains wrong.

3. **The exposed secret must be treated as compromised.**

The important separation is:

and separately:

Do **not** put the secret in `RV_PROFILE`, `APP_PROFILE`, or `ENVIRONMENT`.

I would also remove `APP_PROFILE` and `ENVIRONMENT` entirely from FastAPI Cloud unless you deliberately need them. That leaves one unambiguous profile selector.

Yes — **that is the exact configuration error**.

You currently have:

```text
DATABASE_URL = <supabase>
RV_PROFILE    = <secret key>
```

`RV_PROFILE` **must not contain the secret key**. It is the application environment selector, and RaceVision only accepts values such as `production`, `development`, `staging`, etc.

### Change FastAPI Cloud to

```text
DATABASE_URL = <your existing Supabase database URL>
RV_PROFILE   = production
```

Then your secret belongs in a **separate variable**:

```text
SECRET_KEY = <a new randomly generated secret>
```

Do **not** send me the actual secret.

### Important

Because that secret has already been placed in `RV_PROFILE` and appeared in the deployment logs, **do not move that same value into `SECRET_KEY`**. Generate a new one.

For Windows PowerShell:

```powershell
python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(64))"
```

Copy the generated value directly into:

```text
SECRET_KEY
```

and keep it private.

### So your immediate FastAPI Cloud environment should look conceptually like

```text
DATABASE_URL    <Supabase connection>
RV_PROFILE      production
SECRET_KEY      <NEW generated secret>
```

Leave `DATABASE_URL` as the Supabase integration is providing it.

After saving the variables, **redeploy the application from current `main`**. The old deployment may still be cached/stale.

Once you have changed `RV_PROFILE` to `production` and added the new `SECRET_KEY`, send me the **next FastAPI Cloud startup log** and I’ll check the next failure/success point.
